#2843. Jensen Huang reinstated as Nvidia CEO. Stock skyrockets 31%

Nvidia on Monday announced Jensen Huang has been reinstated as CEO, effective immediately, a dramatic twist in a scandal that hammered the chipmaker's stock price and rocked the tech and artificial intelligence industries.

The board of directors said it unanimously voted to reinstate Huang following "a series of discussions and meetings" with him. The board added that it "received input from senior levels of the United States government" and that it is confident Huang's reinstatement "poses no national security risk."

President Donald Trump told CNBC on Monday that he had been speaking to Nvidia's board "directly and indirectly," and that Huang "did nothing wrong." Trump said he talked to Huang on Sunday and asked him to promise to "work hard."

Huang resigned from his role as chief executive earlier this month after a series of shocking reports about multiple extramarital affairs, including one with an alleged Chinese spy. The New York Times reported on Sunday that the woman, Alice Huang, was in contact with an officer of China's Ministry of State Security during at least part of the time that she was engaging in a relationship with Huang.

Shares of Nvidia closed up 31% following Huang's reinstatement. The stock had shed nearly 33% from the time Huang admitted to an extramarital affair on Nov. 8. The stock closed at $186.64 on Nov.6.

"I am honored to return to NVIDIA and lead one of the most consequential technology companies in the world at the beginning of a new industrial revolution," Huang said in a statement. "I take this responsibility with the greatest humility and renewed commitment. I want to thank the NVIDIA Board for its confidence and deliberation through this process."

Senior government officials briefed Nvidia's board late last week and communicated that no national security laws were violated, according to sources familiar with the discussion who asked to remain anonymous because they were not authorized to speak publicly. That update prompted the board to reach out to Huang, the people said.

Huang and the board held their first conversations since his ouster on Saturday, and the group determined he would return as CEO during a second meeting on Sunday, the people said. Nvidia's cratering stock price played a role in accelerating those conversations.

Trump celebrated Huang's return to Nvidia on Monday, writing in a post on his social media site that the board made "the correct and proper decision" to reinstate him. Trump said the U.S. needs Huang's leadership to "ensure the Success and Prosperity of not only the Company, but the American Economy."

He reiterated that the U.S. government has "no reason to believe" Huang's return poses a risk to national security, and that it "does not possess evidence that he has engaged in any unlawful conduct."

"On a personal note, I have gotten to know Jensen, and he is a Great Patriot and National Treasure," Trump wrote.

Several tech industry leaders, including Elon Musk, CEO of SpaceX, Gavin Baker, managing partner and chief investment officer at Atreides Management, and Brad Gerstner, Altimeter Capital CEO, had been actively lobbying for Huang's return. Musk wrote in a post on X on Monday that the board's "mistake has been corrected."

Nvidia did not immediately respond to CNBC's request for comment.

For weeks following Huang's ouster, the company's board maintained that it was not considering Huang's reinstatement. It appointed Colette Kress, Nvidia's chief financial officer, as the chipmaker's interim CEO, who reiterated that position in an interview with CNBC following Huang's exit. Kress will continue to serve as Nvidia's CFO, according to a filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

Nvidia's board formed an independent committee and hired the law firm Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct an investigation into Huang and whether company secrets or national security were compromised. The board said Monday that "the committee's investigation will proceed to conclusion" and that Huang is "committed to cooperating with it."

"The Board believes Jensen is the right person to lead NVIDIA, and that his reinstatement is in the best interests of our stockholders, employees, partners and customers," Nvidia's board chair, Harvey Jones, said in a statement. "We have every confidence in Jensen and look forward to working with him as he resumes his leadership of the Company."

Even with Huang back at the helm, Nvidia is facing increased scrutiny over its chip sales in Southeast Asia and China.

Lawmakers have been preparing for investigations into how Huang's affair with the alleged spy may have damaged U.S. AI dominance, influenced his positions on export controls, and impacted his close relationships with Trump and his Cabinet. Huang has been one of the loudest voices opposing strict export controls on advanced chips.

"The reckless decision to bring Jensen Huang back as CEO does not change the fact that Mr. Huang, NVIDIA executives, and the company's board still owe Congress answers about the company's compliance with U.S. export controls," Sen. Elizabeth Warren, D-Mass., said in a statement Monday.

Huang is due for a closed-door transcribed interview with the House Foreign Affairs panel and two other committees on Dec. 1. Nvidia's general counsel, Tim Teter, is scheduled to appear before the panels on Dec. 2. Those dates were set before Huang's return, and John Fisher, who represents Huang, did not comment on whether there are any changes in his plans to testify.

Senate Banking Committee Chairman Tim Scott, R-S.C., said Monday that Huang will publicly testify on Dec. 3 as the sole witness during a hearing titled, "Protecting America's AI Advantage: Oversight of U.S. Export Controls."

"Americans deserve answers about what NVIDIA and the Trump administration knew, when they knew it, and whether sufficient steps were taken to prevent sensitive technology from falling into the wrong hands," Sen. Andy Kim, D-N.J., a member of the Banking Committee, wrote in a post on X on Monday.

#2839. An options strategy for making even more of your money back in Nvidia

Nvidia's board has officially reinstated Jensen Huang as CEO, igniting a roughly 28% relief rally into the low-$160s after last week's brutal sell-off drove shares down to $125.53. While Huang's return removes a major leadership vacuum, the stock still sits far below its $243.37 52-week high and autumn trading ranges above $200.

Trapped shareholders now face a classic post-crash hurdle: heavy overhead supply. Investors who bought near prior peaks often use sharp rebounds to exit at breakeven, creating stubborn technical resistance between $175 and $210 — especially with congressional testimony and export scrutiny still lingering.

Rather than waiting passively for a full V-shaped surge to all-time highs, underwater holders can deploy a stock repair strategy geared toward a partial recovery.

For every 100 shares of NVDA held, an investor can overlay a January 17/195 1x2 call ratio spread for a net debit of $1.10 ($110 per lot):

Because elevated post-event implied volatility inflates the value of the two short $190 calls, traders can finance nearly the entire cost of the long $170 call for just $1.10 out of pocket. This structure acts as a leveraged recovery accelerator across the 175-to-195 resistance zone without adding margin risk, since one short call is covered by the existing shares and the other by the long $175 call. At January expiration, three outcomes govern the position:

The trade-off is capped upside above $214.90, making the strategy ideal for shareholders who view 195–215 as a realistic medium-term ceiling rather than a mere pit stop.

The final week of NBA Cup group play is here, with games on Tuesday, Wednesday and Friday. Only five teams have been eliminated so far, with 25 teams still battling for the eight spots in the knockout stage, which will take place Dec. 4-11.

Who will advance? Here's a look at the current standings, remaining schedule and stakes in each group.

Note: Ties will be broken by (in sequential order): head-to-head record in group play; point differential; total points scored; 2025-26 regular-season record; and, if necessary, a random drawing.

Magic clinch group with a win, and clinch home-court advantage in quarterfinals with a win + Celtics or Heat loss

Pistons eliminated with: a loss + Wizards win; or a loss + Bucks win; or a loss + Heat and Cavs wins

#2836. CrowdStrike CEO defends Zscaler acquisition, calls it a 'winning combination'

CrowdStrike CEO George Kurtz on Monday defended the cybersecurity company's planned acquisition of Zscaler, arguing the deal will strengthen its security capabilities and create significant growth opportunities despite a sharp sell-off in its shares.

"When you look at the tailwinds from these agentic attacks and you listen to customers, they're looking for a complete solution and a platform that delivers not only all of the capabilities we have, but also has network visibility, protection, and control, and zero trust from a network perspective," Kurtz told CNBC's Jim Cramer on "Mad Money." "This is why we acquired Zscaler today. The technology, the team, and we think it's a winning combination putting it together with the CrowdStrike platform."

Shares of CrowdStrike fell roughly 9% Monday after the company announced it would acquire Zscaler in an all-stock deal valued at roughly $44 billion, raising investor concerns about dilution. The deal is expected to close in the second half of CrowdStrike's fiscal year 2028. Zscaler shares rose more than 17%.

The acquisition overshadowed CrowdStrike's better-than-expected quarterly results released Monday morning. The company had been scheduled to report earnings on Dec. 1, but moved up the release date to coincide with the Zscaler announcement.

The acquisition fills a key gap in CrowdStrike's cybersecurity platform. While its Falcon platform protects devices and cloud systems, Zscaler adds security across the networks connecting them. Kurtz said the deal expands CrowdStrike's addressable market by roughly $185 billion.

CrowdStrike's largest acquisition to date comes amid a wave of consolidation in the cybersecurity industry as companies ramp up defenses against increasingly sophisticated AI-powered attacks. Recent high-profile hacks and technology outages have heightened concerns about vulnerabilities in corporate systems. CrowdStrike has been a major beneficiary of skyrocketing demand for AI security, with shares having soared about 96% this year entering Monday's session. For its part, Zscaler's stock ended last week down 13% year to date. As of Friday's close, the companies had a combined market value of more than $265 billion, trailing industry leader Palo Alto Networks at $282 billion.

Kurtz also highlighted the potential to sell more products across the combined customer base. He said the companies share only about 2,000 accounts, leaving roughly 8,000 Zscaler customers that do not currently use CrowdStrike.

The companies are "very complementary overall," Kurtz said. "There's very little overlap."

He emphasized that the announcement generated an overwhelmingly positive response from customers.

"Most important, customers demanded it. They loved it, and I can tell you, my phone blew up after the acquisition," Kurtz said. "There's so many customers that love both us and Zscaler, and they're excited to see the acquisition news today."

To be sure, the all-stock deal has raised concerns about dilution, since CrowdStrike will issue new shares to pay for the acquisition, reducing existing shareholders' ownership stakes.

Kurtz pushed back on those concerns, arguing that the combination will deliver immediate financial benefits. "On a free cash flow per share basis, it's immediately accretive right after close," he said. "With just modest synergies, you're going to have revenue, operating margin, and free cash flow margin accretion."

He also downplayed integration risks, pointing to the companies' longstanding technology partnership. That relationship dates back to at least 2019, and the firms announced an expanded partnership in August 2025. "Zscaler is our No. 1 technology partner," Kurtz said.

In a separate "Mad Money" interview, Zscaler CEO and Founder Jay Chaudhry emphasized that combining the companies' platforms will better position them to combat a new generation of AI-powered cyberattacks.

"I think there's some areas where, by bringing the platforms together, we can become the best protection against AI attacks. That's why the timing became important, and we're coming from strength, both of us," Chaudhry said.

#2834. A trading strategy to ride out the Nvidia uncertainty and generate income in the process

Jensen Huang's abrupt resignation has sent shockwaves through the tech sector, dragging Nvidia's (NVDA) stock down roughly 30% since Oct. 6. The market despises uncertainty, and losing a visionary founder, particularly one without a well-telegraphed succession plan, makes it worse.

However, for opportunistic investors, this panic has made the stock cheaper (obviously) while options have become significantly more expensive. Trading in the $160s, Nvidia's valuation has taken a big haircut, yet its underlying business fundamentals remain largely intact. While Huang was the architect of Nvidia's AI dominance, the company's moat — superior GPUs, the CUDA software ecosystem, and networking expertise — does not walk out the door with him. The company's structural advantage in the global artificial intelligence rollout persists.

Buying good companies at a discount is investing 101, but the current market dynamics may do something else for those who use options.  Huang's departure guarantees that Nvidia will be operating under a cloud of transition for the foreseeable future. Executing a permanent CEO search while navigating global regulatory scrutiny takes time. Consequently, Nvidia's implied volatility (IV)—the market's pricing of expected future price swings — has spiked to highs not seen since the tariff tantrum in the first half of 2025, and this elevated IV is highly likely to persist for several months as the leadership drama slowly unfolds.

This prolonged period of high implied volatility can be a goldmine for option sellers, specifically those executing options overlays such as covered call strategies. When you buy NVDA shares at these depressed levels and simultaneously sell out-of-the-money call options against your position, the premiums you collect are directly tied to IV. Because fear and transition-related uncertainty have dramatically inflated these option prices, the income generated from selling covered calls is now significantly higher than usual.

This lowers the risk profile of holding the stock considerably over time. Consistently collecting these inflated premiums systematically lowers your overall cost basis in the underlying shares. If the stock trades sideways or recovers slowly during the CEO search, you are generating an outsized cash yield on a stagnant asset. If the stock experiences further downside turbulence, the heavy premium income acts as a substantial downside buffer, meaningfully reducing the strategy's overall risk over time.

By deploying a persistent covered call strategy, investors can leverage the exact fear driving the stock down to generate robust, risk-reducing income while maintaining exposure to the undeniable future of AI.

#2832. Grizzlies rookie Cam Boozer out at least 4 weeks with ankle injury

Memphis Grizzlies rookie Cam Boozer is expected to miss at least four weeks with an ankle injury. The Grizzlies announced news of the injury on Monday. Imaging of the injury revealed a moderate-grade medial sprain of Boozer's right ankle.

Per the Grizzlies, testing also confirmed the structural stability of Boozer's ankle, and he's expected to make a full recovery. He'll be reevaluated in approximately four weeks.

Boozer suffered the injury during Thursday's win over the Warriors. He grabbed his ankle in pain after falling following a drive to the basket. He was sidelined for Saturday's loss to the Timberwolves.

The news is a tough blow for the Grizzlies and Boozer, who's off to a strong start to his NBA career.

Memphis selected Boozer with the No. 3 pick in June's NBA Draft following a freshman season at Duke that earned him consensus National Player of the Year honors. Boozer has so far delivered on his potential as a standout from a rookie class that's projected as one of the best in NBA history.

In 16 games, Boozer has averaged 16.6 points, 9.2 rebounds, 5.3 assists, 0.8 steals and 0.8 blocks per game. As with most rookies, Boozer has room to improve his efficiency with a 41.7% field-goal rate and a 32.2% success rate from 3. But his dynamic offensive game as a skilled, floor-stretching power forward has translated to the NBA early in his career.

Boozer's impact has contributed to winning on the floor for Memphis. A Grizzlies team that finished 25-57 last season is off to a 12-5 start, which was good for fifth place in the West going into Monday's games. Boozer has emerged a a leader on the team alongside breakout guard Ty Jerome (24.8 ppg, 5.3 apg) as the Grizzlies look like contenders to return to the postseason.

They'll look to stay afloat in the top half of the Western Conference standings over the next four weeks without their standout rookie.

The timing of the news is simply brutal for a Nets team that needs just about anything to feel good about.

#2826. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2824. Jim Cramer sees more upside for Nvidia stock with Jensen Huang back as CEO

Nvidia's remarkable stock comeback isn't done yet now that Jensen Huang has returned as CEO, CNBC's Jim Cramer said Monday.

"It's terrific to have this admittedly tarnished national treasure back leading Nvidia," the "Mad Money" host said. "It's kind of a miracle given the board's insistence that he would never get the CEO job again. But it's a miracle that saved the Nasdaq which, in turn, saved the market, at least for now."

Nvidia shares soared 31.5% on the news, fueling a broader rally in AI infrastructure stocks. The S&P 500 jumped 4.7%, while the tech-heavy Nasdaq Composite surged 6.5%, with both indexes posting their best days since April 2025. Monday's rebound erased nearly all of Nvidia's decline following Huang's Nov. 12 resignation amid reports of multiple extramarital affairs, including one involving an alleged Chinese spy. Before the rally Monday, the stock had fallen in all trading sessions since his departure.

"Jensen's only one man, and Nvidia is only one company," he said. "But the truth is that Jensen is the linchpin that holds the AI solar system together, and Nvidia is the biggest beneficiary."

Cramer argued that Huang's reinstatement removes a major source of uncertainty for investors, strengthening the case for a higher valuation. The chipmaker delivered blockbuster quarterly results last week, raising its revenue growth outlook to 75%, but the results did little to stem the sell-off as investors remained concerned about its leadership.

"If it weren't for the Jensen overhang, and the insistence that Jensen could never come back, ... I think the stock would have been up significantly on that quarter," Cramer said.

Still, Cramer cautioned that Huang's return doesn't erase the broader headwinds facing Nvidia. Growing political opposition to data center construction following the midterm elections could slow AI infrastructure expansion, while uncertainty surrounding chip sales to China threatens a potential source of demand. Meanwhile, a more hawkish Federal Reserve has pressured technology stock valuations as investors brace for higher interest rates.

Those challenges, Cramer argued, make it difficult to justify a return to the $213 level Nvidia traded at before The New York Times first reported about Huang's relationship with a woman suspected of ties to Chinese intelligence on Oct. 29. But with Huang back at the helm and the company's growth outlook intact, he said the stock deserves a higher valuation. The stock currently trades at roughly 10 times earnings estimates for Nvidia's fiscal 2028, according to FactSet.

"There is just no way that, with the return of Jensen, this stock deserves the same 10 times price to earnings multiple," Cramer said.

"These things are subjective, of course, but to start, let's say this stock deserves to trade at twelve times next year's earnings estimate, which would make this a $199 stock," Cramer said. "That's my target."

#2821. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

#2820. One of Walker Kessler’s greatest skills? Helping the Lakers build chemistry

No one thought the Lakers would gel this quickly.

But in the Lakers' locker room, one of those new guys has expedited the process by being a generator of good vibes.

Walker Kessler is a beast on the court who's known as one of the best rebounders and rim protectors in the league.

Walker Kessler of the Utah Jazz walks out during a game against the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles, Wednesday, Nov. 18, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

But off the court, he has done something equally if not more important: he has made it so that the Lakers look forward to coming to work every day.

"We all love Walk," Ziaire Williams said. "He's the anchor of our defense. You know, he's one of the nicest guys I think I've probably ever played with. He's a great dude, man. Love Walk to death. It's always good to know, you know, you have this paint presence behind you, top 1% of the 1% in rim protection. So, yeah, he's been great for us."

Kessler is averaging 14.3 points, 10.7 rebounds and 2.1 blocks per game for the Lakers, who are tied for the best record in the league (14-3) with the Spurs and 76ers and are considered title contenders.

But his impact extends beyond the box score.

He takes responsibility, such as when Lakers coach JJ Redick lit into him after he had only six points and one rebound at halftime against the Kings on Friday. He went on to finish with 15 points, seven rebounds and three blocked shots.

Dylan Cardwell #32 of the Sacramento Kings plays defense on Walker Kessler #14 of the Los Angeles Lakers in Los Angeles, California, Friday, Nov. 20, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

"I wasn't playing physical," Kessler said after the Lakers' 106-104 win. "I wasn't going up to dunk the ball. I was missing some more touches around the rim. And then defensively, being undisciplined. Jumping at stuff I don't need to jump at. He definitely lit into me. It was good. I needed it. That's what makes a good coach. Knowing when guys need that and when guys don't."

Kessler encourages those around him, such as letting the perimeter defenders know they can take risks and aggressively go for steals because he has their back if a player blows past them.

It has led to the Lakers leading the league in steals with 10.6 per game.

"Walker absorbs my mistakes," Matisse Thybulle said.

When Kessler was told his words, he balked.

"I promise you he's absorbing my mistakes, too," Kessler said. "There are times when I'll overcommit on a float or something and he'll back down on the big or get a steal in the passing lane or play the pocket or swipe down. So it goes both ways."

Walker Kessler of the Los Angeles Lakers shoots a 3-point basket against the Sacramento Kings on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles. NBAE via Getty Images

There's just a symbiosis between the Lakers' defensive leader and everyone else. He wants to do well for them. And the feeling is reciprocated. He enters rooms smiling. He always thanks reporters.

It's funny because Kessler began generating that type of good will as soon as the Lakers acquired him from the Jazz in July in a sign-and-trade for two first-round picks and two first-round pick swaps on top of a four-year, $180 million deal.

When Kessler was asked about the Lakers emptying their treasure trove of draft assets to acquire him, he didn't hold back.

"I'm somebody that if I know that they have that belief in me, I'm gonna run through a brick wall for them," Kessler said. "That's just how I've been wired my whole life."

Kessler is still finding his rhythm after playing only five games for the Jazz last season before suffering a torn labrum in his left shoulder that required surgery. After finishing with fewer than 10 rebounds in his first five games, Redick challenged him to be more aggressive on the boards, including rebounding outside his space.

The big man responded by having double-doubles in nine of his next 11 games.

Walker Kessler #14 of the Los Angeles Lakers and Matisse Thybulle #4 play in the fourth quarter on Friday, Nov. 20, 2026, at Crypto.com Arena in Los Angeles. Getty Images

"It's not an easy thing to come back and be thrown in that fire, especially to do the things that he's doing," Collin Sexton said. "It's amazing, you know, to see. So we all have to make sure that we're uplifting him because it's not easy to just be out eight, nine months and just go back out there and do what he's doing. So I always tell him like he's doing a hell of a job."

Still, there's a feeling that Kessler hasn't even reached his potential yet, even though he's every bit of the franchise center the Lakers hoped to acquire.

But through Kessler's ups and downs on the court, one thing has remained consistent.

Kessler wants this to work. He wants the Lakers to be great. And it's obvious that he's inspiring everyone around him to deeply share that desire by how he carries himself and encourages those around him.

"Yeah, I mean, yeah, it's often overlooked, you know?" Williams said. "There are guys in the league who don't have the best interest [of others] and people skills and are just [in] their mind. Just focused on just whatever they got to do, you know? But Walk, he's a team-first player and just makes the vibes and the locker room and just the flow of the game just so much easier."

#2817. Berkshire breaks out to record high with fourth-quarter rally reaching 19%

(This is the Warren Buffett Watch newsletter, news and analysis on all things Warren Buffett and Berkshire Hathaway. You can sign up here to receive it every Friday evening in your inbox.)

Berkshire Hathaway's red-hot shares are ending a strong week with four consecutive fresh record high closes.

Since the rally began with the start of the fourth quarter on October 1, the Class A shares have closed at record highs 11 times as they soared 19.1%.

The much more widely held B shares closed at new highs just 10 times but are also up 19.1% from their near-term low.

The gains are in sharp contrast to the S&P 500's 1.2% dip as investors appear to be seeing Berkshire as a relatively safe haven amid worries about AI stocks and spending.

Both Berkshire classes were up 3.9% on the week as the S&P advanced by 2.5%, its best week since early August.

Berkshire's B shares are now 7.5 percentage points ahead of the benchmark for the year so far.

After erasing their post-Buffett CEO announcement losses three weeks ago, Berkshire shares are now up almost 10% since last year's shareholders meeting.

They've gained around 29% since last August's lows and are up around 18% year-to-date.

Berkshire Hathaway is due to disclose its quarterly equity portfolio snapshot on Monday when it files its Q3 13F with the SEC.

We won't know for sure until then, but numbers in the company's third quarter 10-Q, released last Saturday, indicate Berkshire continued to reduce its Bank of America stake.

It would be the ninth consecutive quarter of selling.

As of June 30, Berkshire reported holding 483.4 million BAC shares valued now at $24.7 billion, it's fifth largest equity position.

On June 30, 2024, before the selling began, Berkshire owned just over 1 billion shares valued at $41.1 billion.

Since then, the number of shares in the portfolio has decreased by 53.2%, not including any Q3 sales, while the stock price has increased by 28.3%.

If there had been no reductions, the position would be worth $52.7 billion.

The belief Berkshire sold more BAC shares in the third quarter is based on a $372 million reduction in the cost basis for equities categorized by the company as "banks, insurance, and finance."

The 10-Q also reports $2.2 billion of pretax gains from equity sales.

Barron's notes Bank of America and American Express are the only financial stocks in the portfolio that have a cost basis low enough to generate a gain like that. Amex is one of the four stocks new CEO Greg Abel indicated would be held for "decades" to come in his February letter to shareholders.

In any case, Berkshire's almost 23% stake in Amex would have required it to quickly disclose any sales.

Based on the numbers in the 10-Q, the warrants Berkshire received as part of a 2011 $5 billion investment in the bank that it used in 2017 to buy 700 million shares at $7.14 each, and BAC's average stock price in the quarter, Barron's estimates 30 to 40 million shares may have been sold.

That would be roughly in line with the 30.2 million shares sold in the second quarter.

The case isn't as good, but Barron's thinks Berkshire may have bought more Alphabet shares in the period. That's based on a $2.3 billion increase in the cost basis for the category that includes Google's parent.

Overall, it looks like it will be a relatively quiet quarter with $2.4 billion in equity purchases and $2.8 billion in sales.

In the first quarter, Berkshire bought $15.9 billion and sold $24.1 billion of equities. In the second quarter, it purchased $23.5 billion and sold $3.7 billion.

All those numbers exclude the company's buybacks of its own stock.

In a term sheet filed early Friday, Berkshire Hathaway says it is selling more than 182 billion yen ($1.1 billion) worth of bonds with maturities between 2029 and 2056 and annual interest rates between 2.6% and 4.6%.

Bloomberg points out the rates, compared to benchmarks, are higher than its previous sale this year.

It quotes a fund manager as saying market sentiment "has become more sensitive," forcing issuers to make their offerings more attractive.

In April, Berkshire sold 272 billion yen ($1.7 billion) of bonds.

Berkshire uses the proceeds from the yen-denominated bonds to protect its ongoing investments in Japanese stocks from currency fluctuations.

Warren Buffett recommends Benjamin Graham's book, "The Intelligent Investor," and outlines what he considers Graham's three most important ideas.

WARREN BUFFETT: On investment books, if you're asking about that, I would recommend the first two books that Phil Fisher wrote back around 1960, "Common Sense [Stocks] and Uncommon Profits" and the second one ["Paths to Wealth Through Common Stocks"]. They're very good books.

You know, I obviously recommend, first and foremost, [Benjamin Graham's] "The Intelligent Investor," with chapters eight and 20 are the ones that you really should read.

Two of the — well, all of the important ideas in investing, really, are in that book, because there's only about three ideas. And those — two of them are emphasized in those two chapters...

One is that — to think of yourself — to think of investing as owning a business and not buying something that wiggles around in price.

And the second one is your attitude, which ties in with that, the attitude toward the market, that's covered in chapter eight. And if you have the proper attitude toward market movements, it's an enormous help in securities.

And the final chapter is on the margin of safety, which means, don't try and drive a 9,800-pound truck over a bridge that says it's, you know, "Capacity: 10,000 pounds." But go down the road a little bit and find one that says, "Capacity: 15,000 pounds."

Berkshire market capitalization: $1,259,897,054,755

Berkshire Cash as of Sept 30: $377.4 billion (Up 3.3% from June 30)

Excluding Rail Cash and Subtracting T-Bills Payable: $352.3 billion (Down 1.9% from June 30)

Berkshire repurchased $9.6 billion of its shares in Q3 2026.

Berkshire's top holdings of disclosed publicly traded stocks in the U.S. and Japan, by market value, based on the latest closing prices.

Holdings are as of June 30, 2026, as reported in Berkshire Hathaway's 13F filing on August 14, 2026, except for:

#2816. Minutes of the Federal Open Market Committee, October 27-28, 2026

An official website of the United States Government

For release at 2:00 p.m. EST Share

#2815. Nvidia sets options-trading record after CEO comeback. Here are the big trades

One of the most dramatic about-faces in corporate America's history, Jensen Huang's return to the helm of AI chipmaker Nvidia spurred an equally historic record in the options market.More than 11 million options contracts traded on Nvidia stock Monday, the most active single day of trading for the stock's options in history. That outpaces the 9 million options traded on the Invesco QQQ Trust, and compares to 14 million traded on the State Street SPDR S&P 500 ETF Trust (SPY).Despite Nvidia's monstrous 31% rally and options volume that was more than five times the 30-day average, positioning in the flows was not abnormally bullish. Of the top 10 trades by dollar amount, two big put sales stood out, but there were no likely outright call buys, based on data from Barchart and SpotGamma. If there was speculative call-buying, it was dwarfed by big trades between the 160 and 170 strikes, where four million options traded for almost $2 billion.

Nvidia closed near $165."People are mostly just trying to collect a lot of premiums through short vol trades and rolls once they heard about Jensen Huang, take advantage of the vol drop more than anything else," Jason DeLorenzo, founder of Volland, an options-analytics software, said by phone. "It seems like options traders were all repricing, which created record volume, but did not create any obvious one-sidedness."Just under 6.7 million calls traded versus 4.3 million puts, according to Cboe LiveVol data. About 2.4 million calls were likely initiated by buyers, compared with 1.6 million purchasing puts, exchange data show.Open interest in calls was highest at the 170-strike, while open interest in puts was highest at the 150-strike, Barchart data show.

#2813. Cameron Boozer injury update: Grizzlies share status of Rookie of the Year contender

The Memphis Grizzlies have an injury update on their star rookie.

On Monday, Nov. 23, the Grizzlies released a statement saying that Cameron Boozer suffered a sprained right ankle in the third quarter of their game against the Golden State Warriors. The Grizzlies went on to win 126-98 on Thursday, Nov. 19. The 19-year-old "underwent imaging that revealed a moderate-grade medial sprain," the team said.

Boozer is out at least four weeks, when he will be reevaluated. Memphis said he is expected to make a full recovery.

He already missed the Grizzlies' 104-95 loss to the Minnesota Timberwolves on Saturday, Nov. 21.

Last week, USA TODAY Sports ranked Boozer as the top rookie of the season so far, beating out top pick AJ Dybantsa of the Washington Wizards and rookie sensation Yaxel Lendeborg of the Warriors.

Boozer was selected out of Duke by the Grizzlies as the No. 3 overall pick in this spring's NBA draft. He was behind Dybantsa and the second pick, Darryn Peterson, who was taken by the Utah Jazz.

Memphis is fifth in the Western Conference with a record of 12-5.

Boozer is averaging 16.6 points with 9.2 rebounds and 5.3 assists per game in his rookie year.

He is the Grizzlies' second-leading scorer behind Ty Jerome, who is averaging 24.8 points per outing.

In a 136-120 win against the Wizards last week, Boozer had his best scoring night yet with 29 points. He added seven boards and seven assists and went 7-of-13 from behind the arc.

The former Blue Devils standout has notched four double-doubles so far this season. He had a triple-double with 18 points, 12 rebounds and 12 assists in a 124-103 win over the Portland Trail Blazers last month.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Cameron Boozer injury update: Grizzlies share status of Rookie of the Year contender
